user
You are the second reviewer on a routine security code review of our own
nix-bitcoin fork: the NixOS modules and packaging we deploy on our own Bitcoin
and Lightning node. Two prior reviewers have already produced the findings
below against the exact commit checked out in the current directory. Your job
is quality control on their work, as the maintainer's independent check:

For EACH finding, open the cited file at the cited location and decide:
- verdict: "confirmed" (the defect is real as described), "false-positive"
  (the code does not do what the finding claims, or an existing control
  already prevents it — name the control), or "needs-info" (cannot be decided
  from the tree; say what is missing).
- severity: your own call (critical/high/medium/low/info), which may differ
  from the reviewer's.
- reasoning: one to three sentences grounded in the code you read (quote the
  line or construct that decides it).
- fix_ok: whether the reviewer's recommended fix is correct and complete
  (true/false) and, if false, what is wrong with it.

Then, only for the files the findings touch, note up to three defects the
reviewers missed or misread that a maintainer should know about ("missed").
Do not pad: if there is nothing, say so.

Read-only tools only; do not modify the tree. Be terse and concrete.

End your answer with exactly one fenced ```json block of this shape:
{"reviews":[{"id":"<finding id or title>","verdict":"confirmed|false-positive|needs-info","severity":"...","reasoning":"...","fix_ok":true,"fix_note":""}],
 "missed":[{"file":"...","line":0,"severity":"...","title":"...","description":"..."}],
 "summary":"two or three sentences"}
followed by nothing else.

name: lean-ctx
description: Context Engineering for AI Agents — 81 MCP tools, 10 read modes, 95+ shell patterns, tree-sitter AST for 27 languages. Compresses LLM context by up to 99%. Use when reading files, running shell commands, searching code, or exploring directories. Auto-installs if not present.

# lean-ctx — Context Engineering for AI Agents

## Setup

```bash
which lean-ctx || curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yvgude/lean-ctx/main/skills/lean-ctx/scripts/install.sh | bash
lean-ctx setup
```

## Core Tools (10 always visible)

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `ctx_read(path, mode)` | Read file with compression and caching |
| `ctx_search(pattern, path)` | Search code with compressed results |
| `ctx_shell(command)` | Run shell with compressed output |
| `ctx_tree(path, depth)` | Directory listing |
| `ctx_patch(path, ops)` | Anchored editing (line+hash, no old-text echo) |
| `ctx_session(action)` | Session state and persistence |
| `ctx_knowledge(action)` | Project knowledge across sessions |
| `ctx_overview(task)` | Task-relevant project map |
| `ctx_graph(action)` | Code relationships and impact |
| `ctx_call(name, args)` | Invoke any tool by name |

## Shell Hook (use instead of raw exec)

```bash
lean-ctx -c "git status"
lean-ctx -c "cargo test"
lean-ctx -c "npm install"
lean-ctx ls src/
```

## ctx_read Modes

| Mode | When |
|------|------|
| `anchored` | Files you will edit (full text + `N:hh\|` anchors for ctx_patch) |
| `full` | Verbatim cached read |
| `map` | Context-only (deps + exports) |
| `signatures` | API surface only |
| `diff` | After edits (changed lines) |
| `aggressive` | Large files, syntax-stripped; JSON arrays row-deduped (lossless) |
| `entropy` | Shannon filtering |
| `task` | Task-relevant lines |
| `lines:N-M` | Specific range |
| `auto` | System selects optimal |

Re-reads cost ~13 tokens. fresh=true bypasses cache.
Redundant JSON (arrays of like objects) is crushed losslessly into a compact
`_defaults` + per-row form; if a slice was dropped, recover it with
`ctx_expand(id, json_path=… | search=…)`.

## File Editing

Anchored editing saves output tokens: `ctx_read(mode="anchored")` → `ctx_patch(path, op, line, hash, new_text)`.
Never reproduce old text byte-for-byte; batch via `ops:[…]`; `op=create` writes new files.
Stale anchor → CONFLICT with fresh anchors (retry once). Native Edit/StrReplace stay fine;
`ctx_edit` (str_replace) is the legacy fallback via ctx_call/power profile.

## More Tools (via ctx_call or ctx_load_tools)

Architecture: ctx_symbol, ctx_callgraph, ctx_impact, ctx_architecture, ctx_routes, ctx_smells, ctx_quality
  ↳ "What breaks if I change this file/class/type?" → ctx_impact (file-level blast radius; resolves same-package/namespace type usage with no import for C#, Java, Go and Kotlin). "Who calls this function?" → ctx_callgraph (symbol-level). "How navigable / how much is complexity costing me?" → ctx_quality (navigability score + token quality tax).
Multi-agent: ctx_agent, ctx_share, ctx_task, ctx_handoff, ctx_workflow
Verify: ctx_benchmark, ctx_verify, ctx_proof, ctx_review
Batch: ctx_fill, ctx_execute, ctx_expand, ctx_pack

Full docs: https://leanctx.com/docs

437:        # service in that group gets full privileged RPC. Keep group membership
438:        # minimal; new services should use an rpc user + HMAC instead (audit L-10).
439:        # Use an explicit 0640 rather than `chmod g=r`: the latter leaves the
440:        # other-read bit at whatever bitcoind created (its umask), so the cookie's
441:        # world-readability would hinge on the app's file mode. 0640 clears it.

3:# Create a WireGuard server with a single peer.
4:# Private/public keys are created via the secrets system.
5:# Add helper binaries `nix-bitcoin-wg-connect` and optionally `lndconnect-wg`.
6:
7:# See ../../docs/services.md ("Use Zeus (mobile lightning wallet) via WireGuard")
8:# for usage instructions.
9:
10:# This is a rather opinionated implementation that lacks the flexibility offered by
11:# other nix-bitcoin modules, so ship this as a `preset`.
12:# Some users will prefer to use `lndconnect` with their existing WireGuard or Tailscale setup.
13:
14:with lib;
15:let
16:  options.nix-bitcoin.wireguard = {
17:    subnet = mkOption {
18:      type = types.str;
19:      default = "10.10.0";
20:      description = "The /24 subnet of the wireguard network.";
21:    };
22:    restrictPeer = mkOption {
23:      type = types.bool;
24:      default = true;
25:      description = ''
26:        Prevent the peer from connecting to any addresses except for the WireGuard server address.
27:      '';
28:    };
29:  };
30:
31:  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.wireguard;
32:  wgSubnet = cfg.subnet;

48:  # The QR code encodes a wg-quick config that can be imported by the wireguard

61:          ;;
62:      esac
63:    done

67:      # This internally uses https://github.com/GlenDC/go-external-ip, which
68:      # queries a set of external ip providers.

96:  config = {
97:    assertions = [
98:      {
99:        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
100:        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
101:        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
102:      }
103:      # The whole security model of this preset is the iptables rule set
104:      # below: the lnd admin REST listener is bound to 0.0.0.0 (see the comment
105:      # at `restAddress`) and the peer restriction is an iptables REJECT. Two
106:      # silent ways to lose that (audit 2026-09-09): the firewall disabled, or
107:      # the nftables backend enabled — NixOS then ignores `extraCommands`
108:      # without an error, so the REST accept rule and the restrictPeer REJECT
109:      # simply vanish. Refuse to evaluate rather than ship an unguarded admin API.
110:      {
111:        # restrictPeer is also only an iptables REJECT: with the firewall off it
112:        # evaluates fine and installs nothing (audit second opinion 2026-10-01).
113:        assertion = !(lndconnect || cfg.restrictPeer) || config.networking.firewall.enable;
114:        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: services.lnd.lndconnect binds the admin REST API to 0.0.0.0 and `restrictPeer` is an iptables REJECT; both rely on networking.firewall — enable the firewall.";
115:      }
116:      {
117:        # Check the effective backend, not the enable flags that feed its
118:        # default: `networking.firewall.backend` can also be set directly, and
119:        # both the nftables and the firewalld backends drop `extraCommands`
120:        # without an error (audits 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-04).
121:        assertion = (config.networking.firewall.backend or "iptables") == "iptables";
122:        message = "nix-bitcoin wireguard preset: its rules are iptables `extraCommands`, which the nftables and firewalld backends silently drop (networking.firewall.backend = \"${config.networking.firewall.backend or "iptables"}\"). Keep the iptables backend or port the rules.";

134:          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
135:          # at peer service runtime.

137:          allowedIPs = [ "${peerAddress}/32" ];

148:      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
149:      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
150:      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
151:      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
152:        script = mkForce ''

154:        '';

157:        '';

169:    networking.firewall = let
170:      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
171:    in {
172:      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
173:
174:      extraCommands =
175:        optionalString lndconnect ''
176:          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
177:          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
178:          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
179:          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
180:          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
181:          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
182:        ''
183:        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
184:          # Insert at the top: appended (-A) the REJECT would sit behind the
185:          # standard accept rules (allowed ports, the REST accept above), so a
186:          # peer could still reach any firewall-allowed port on the node's
187:          # other addresses through the tunnel (second opinion 2026-09-09).
188:          # Replies to server-initiated traffic are unaffected: their
189:          # destination is serverAddress.
190:          iptables -w -I nixos-fw 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
191:          iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
192:        '';
193:
194:      extraStopCommands =
195:        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
196:        # the NixOS firewall service.
197:        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
198:          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
199:        '';
200:    };
201:
202:    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
203:    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
204:    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
205:    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
206:    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
207:    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
208:    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
209:    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
210:    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
211:    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
212:    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
213:      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
214:      tor.enforce = false;
215:    };

218:      wg-server-private-key = {};

221:      wg-peer-public-key = {};

227:      makeWireguardKey() {
228:        local name=$1
229:        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
230:        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
231:        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
232:          ${wg} genkey > $priv
233:        fi
234:        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
235:          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
236:        fi
237:      }

239:      makeWireguardKey peer

252:        User = cfg.user;

258:          curl = "${pkgs.curl}/bin/curl -fsS --cacert ${cfg.certPath}";
259:          restUrl = "https://${nbLib.addressWithPort cfg.restAddress cfg.restPort}/v1";
260:          # Setting macaroon permissions for other users needs root permissions
261:          # The admin macaroon is passed to curl via a fd because argv is
262:          # world-readable through /proc/<pid>/cmdline
263:          script = nbLib.rootScript "lnd-create-macaroons" ''
264:            umask ug=r,o=
265:            # $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY (/run/lnd) is writable by the lnd service user,
266:            # so this root script must never write/chmod/chown through a path the
267:            # lnd process could tamper with (else a compromised lnd escalates to
268:            # arbitrary root file overwrite/chown). Staging in $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY
269:            # is NOT enough — lnd owns that directory and can unlink our temp file
270:            # and swap in a symlink before we write to it. Stage instead in a
271:            # ROOT-ONLY directory (mode 0700, owned by root, that lnd cannot enter
272:            # or replace) on the SAME tmpfs as /run/lnd, so the final move is a
273:            # rename(2): atomic, and it replaces the destination without following
274:            # a symlink lnd may have planted there.
275:            staging=$(${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mktemp -d "/run/lnd-macaroons.XXXXXX")
276:            trap '${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/rm -rf "$staging"' EXIT
277:            ${lib.concatMapStrings (macaroon: ''
278:              echo "Create custom macaroon ${macaroon}"
279:              macaroonTmp="$staging/${macaroon}.macaroon"
280:              # Read admin.macaroon AS the lnd user, never as root: it lives in
281:              # lnd's own 0770 datadir, so a compromised lnd could plant a
282:              # symlink there and have this root script dereference it to
283:              # exfiltrate an arbitrary root-only file (backup passphrase, wg
284:              # key, host keys) via the REST call below. Reading as the lnd user
285:              # fails closed on such a symlink — lnd cannot read what lnd cannot
286:              # read — and grants no access lnd does not already have. (The write
287:              # path above is already symlink-hardened; this closes the read.)
288:              adminMacaroonHex=$(${pkgs.util-linux}/bin/runuser -u ${cfg.user} -- ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -ps -u -c 99999 '${networkDir}/admin.macaroon')
289:              # The response body is lnd-controlled. A macaroon is a few hundred
290:              # bytes, so cap what this root script will buffer and write to the
291:              # /run tmpfs at 64 KiB, and fail (jq -e) rather than write garbage
292:              # when the reply carries no `.macaroon` (audit 2026-08-30, low).
293:              ${curl} \
294:                -H @<(printf 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: %s\n' "$adminMacaroonHex") \
295:                -X POST \
296:                -d '{"permissions":[${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.permissions}]}' \
297:                ${restUrl}/macaroon |\
298:                ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/head -c 65536 |\
299:                ${pkgs.jq}/bin/jq -ce '.macaroon | strings' | ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -p -r > "$macaroonTmp"
300:              chown ${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.user}: "$macaroonTmp"
301:              # -T: the destination is a path lnd controls; without -T a
302:              # symlink-to-directory planted there would make root move the
303:              # file INTO that directory under the fixed basename instead of
304:              # replacing the path (second opinion 2026-09-09).
305:              ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mv -fT -- "$macaroonTmp" "$RUNTIME_DIRECTORY/${macaroon}.macaroon"
306:            '') (attrNames cfg.macaroons)}
307:          '';
308:        in [
309:          script
310:        ];
311:      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce;

 succeeded in 391ms:
1:# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
2:
3:Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
4:
5:The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
6:that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
7:(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
8:interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
9:accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
10:The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
11:10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
12:things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
13:
14:> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
15:> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
16:> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
17:> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
18:
19:## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
20:
21:1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
22:   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
23:   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
24:   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
25:   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
26:   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
27:   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
28:   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
29:   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
30:   auditor trade-off of its own).
31:
32:2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
33:   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
34:   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
35:   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
36:   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
37:   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
38:   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
39:   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
40:   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
41:   must tolerate rotation.
42:
43:3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
44:   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
45:   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
46:   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
47:   the daemon is healthy.
48:
49:4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
50:   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
51:   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
52:   wg-dependent.
53:
54:5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
55:   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
56:   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
57:
58:6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
59:   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
60:
61:## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
62:
63:Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
64:the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
65:entries:
66:
67:```nix
68:services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
69:  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
70:'';
71:```
72:
73:All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
74:wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
75:targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
76:dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
77:
78:- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
79:  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
80:  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
81:- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
82:  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
83:  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
84:  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
85:  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
86:
87:## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
88:
89:- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
90:- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
91:      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
92:      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
93:      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
94:      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
95:      connects.
96:- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
97:- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
98:      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
99:      existing clients.
100:- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
101:      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
102:      items 1–6.

Also two crash DoS fixed in 26.06: `setconfig` (PR #8751), xpay circular routehints (PR #9174). | github.com/ElementsProject/lightning |
20:| 3 | **joinmarket** | 0.9.11 | **0.9.12** | MEDIUM | Commitments not format-checked before comparison: malicious taker bypasses anti-snooping, harvests maker UTXO info over time. Upstream calls the fix "very important". Project is now ARCHIVED — no future security updates. Plan migration. | joinmarket-clientserver release-notes-0.9.12 |
21:| 4 | **RTL** | 0.15.8 | **0.15.10** | MEDIUM | Auth request validation hardening (PR #1654, 2FA setups most affected); auth secrets leaked into node logs and config API responses (PR #1659/#1664). 0.15.9 separately cleared 13 npm audit findings (2 critical) via request→axios, csurf→csrf-csrf. | github.com/Ride-The-Lightning/RTL |
22:| 5 | **bitcoind_29** (fallback pkg) | 29.2 | 29.4 | LOW-MED | Two point releases behind. Core's low-severity disclosure policy means low-sev fixes ship silently in point releases. Default `bitcoind` (31.1 from nixpkgs) is unaffected. Bump to 29.4 or drop the fallback. | bitcoincore.org |
23:| 6 | **bitcoin-knots** | 29.3.knots20260508 | 29.4.knots20260508 (2026-08-07) | LOW-MED | One point release behind; 29.4.knots carries the Core 29.4 fixes. | github.com/bitcoinknots/bitcoin |
24:| 7 | **lightning-loop** | 0.33.2-beta | 0.34.0-beta | LOW | 0.34.0 bumps transitive Go deps "to address security alerts". | lightninglabs/loop |

50:## 3. nix-bitcoin's own code — module findings
51:
52:### High
53:
54:**M-1. clightning-rest: full-admin REST API bound to 0.0.0.0 by default** — `modules/clightning-rest.nix:45,60`
55:`RPCCOMMANDS=["*"]` (includes `withdraw`) and listens on all interfaces. Enabling the module exposes
56:complete wallet control to any network the node sits on. Fix: default `rpcCommands` to a read-only
57:set and bind localhost unless an explicit option opts into exposure.
58:
59:**M-2. lndconnect without onion rebinds admin APIs to 0.0.0.0** — `modules/lndconnect.nix:177,213`
60:Enabling lndconnect (for wallet QR pairing) silently moves the lnd REST API (and clnrest, where
61:applicable) from localhost/Tor to all interfaces. These are macaroon/rune-authenticated admin
62:endpoints; exposure should never be a side effect of a QR-code helper. Fix: keep services on
63:localhost and have lndconnect print the onion path, or require an explicit `openFirewall`-style flag.
64:
65:### Medium
66:
67:**M-3. bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist is not DoS-safe** — `modules/bitcoind-rpc-public-whitelist.nix`
68:The "public" set includes `getblocktemplate` and `scantxoutset` (expensive, unauthenticated DoS via
69:the public RPC proxy), plus peer-info and wallet-UTXO leaks (`listunspent`-class). Fix: split into
70:`safe` (getblockchaininfo, estimatesmartfee, ...) vs `expensive`, expose only `safe` by default.
71:
72:**M-4. onion-addresses follows symlinks in user-owned dirs as root** — `modules/onion-addresses.nix:86-105`
73:Root service with CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE reads hostname files from service-owned data dirs without
74:symlink/hardlink checks; also unquoted shell expansions. A compromised service user could redirect
75:reads (e.g. wire a macaroon path into a world-readable onion-address output). Fix: `test ! -L` guards,
76:quote everything, consider `ProtectSystem=strict` + per-service read-only bind instead.
77:
78:**M-5. clightning-replication mounts service lacks all systemd hardening** — `modules/clightning-replication.nix:159-200`
79:Every other daemon gets `defaultHardening` (ProtectSystem=strict, NoNewPrivileges, PrivateTmp, ...);
80:the replication mount helper does not. Also a doc/code mismatch on the SSH key filename. Fix: apply
81:`defaultHardening` with the minimal mount exceptions.
82:
83:**M-6. netns-exec execvp with inherited PATH/env** — `pkgs/netns-exec/`
84:The capability wrapper (post-#846: owner-only 0500 + cap_sys_admin, setns into joinmarket netns) calls
85:`execvp` with the caller's environment and does not require an absolute path. PATH manipulation by the
86:operator could run an unexpected binary inside the netns; unchecked `cap_*` returns and a missing
87:exit-status on exec failure are secondary. Operator is already privileged, so practical impact is
88:contained. Fix: require `argv[0]` absolute, scrub env, check returns.
89:
90:**M-7. ecdsa CVE-2024-23342 (Minerva timing attack) meta stripped repo-wide** — `overlay.nix` / `pkgs/`
91:The CVE flag on the python `ecdsa` package is removed globally to re-enable `hwi`/`trezor` (hardware
92:wallet support). Plausible for Miniscript-only paths, but the unlock is blanket: any consumer doing
93:ecdsa signing is silently re-exposed. Fix: scope the meta rewrite to the two packages that need it,
94:with a re-check date.
95:
96:**M-8. txzmq 0.8.2 (2019-era, unmaintained)** — `pkgs/python-packages/txzmq/`
97:Custom-packaged for the clightning zmq plugin. No known CVE, but dead upstream on an old Twisted.
98:Fix: consider dropping the zmq plugin, or budget maintenance for the fork.
99:
100:### Low (fix opportunistically)
101:
102:- `modules/secrets/secrets.nix:80` — RPC password passed in process argv during secret generation (visible in `/proc` briefly). Pass via stdin/env.
103:- `modules/secrets/secrets.nix:166-183` — unquoted config values/filenames in a root shell script; `printf '%q'` or env passing.
104:- `modules/presets/wireguard.nix:101-109` — root service re-executes its own script with a pubkey spliced in by `sed`. Converts secrets-store write into root RCE; fragile. Use `wg set` directly.
105:- `modules/clightning.nix:172` + `modules/clnrest.nix:57` — group `clightning` (operator, btcpayserver) can read the **admin** rune: convenience access and "withdraw all funds" share one boundary. Issue a read-only restricted rune for operator/RTL/nodeinfo; keep admin 0600.
106:- `modules/bitcoind.nix:302` — `.cookie` chmod g=r: group `bitcoin` == full privileged RPC. Prefer the dedicated `bitcoinrpc-public`-style group.
107:- `modules/presets/bitcoind-remote.nix:13-15` — privileged `rpcpassword` appended to bitcoin.conf on every restart (file grows), in a 0640 group-readable file. Rewrite instead of append; 0600.
108:- `pkgs/krops` — fetchgit pins a **tag** (re-targetable), hash still protects integrity. Pin the commit.
109:- `doCheck = false` on liquid-swap, sha256, chromalog, autobahn — re-enable where feasible (liquid-swap moves funds).
110:- `pkgs/rtl` — `--legacy-peer-deps` (tracked TODO-EXTERNAL, RTL#1182).
111:- `pkgs/nixops` — NixOps 1.x is EOL; dev-only tooling, prefer krops/morph.
112:
113:### Verified good (no action)
114:
115:- PR #846 netns-exec permission fix is present and complete: owner-only `0500`, file-cap `cap_sys_admin=ep`, no setuid, exact-string whitelist of `nb-joinmarket`, caps dropped before exec.
116:- No secrets in `/nix/store`. Secret files are assembled in preStart with `install -m 600/640`, `umask u=rw,go=`, and setup-secrets locks unlisted files to `root:root 0440`.
117:- All source fetches in pkgs/ are hash-pinned, HTTPS-only, no curl|bash, no remote patches, no prebuilt binaries. `bitcoind_29` additionally does full GPG multi-key verification of SHA256SUMS against a hash-pinned guix.sigs keyring — exemplary.
118:- All onion services are v3 with correct port mapping.
119:- `defaultHardening` (pkgs/lib.nix) is applied to every daemon service; clightning-replication (M-5) is the only exception found.
120:- helper/push-release.sh: token from `pass`, GPG-signed artifacts, signature verified against pinned fingerprint on fetch.
121:

127:this audit by deletion. Status of every finding:

139:| M-3 public RPC whitelist | FIXED | Dropped the DoS/side-effect methods (scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate, getblockfrompeer); whitelist now mkDefault-overridable. getpeerinfo/getnodeaddresses are KEPT — the "public" user is also lnd/nbxplorer's local RPC user and they require them (removing them fails lnd's chain-backend health check). 161:## 5. Maintenance posture (the real long-term risk)
162:
163:The snapshot is fresh (nixpkgs pins are ~5 days old), but the security model of this repo is
164:**rolling nixpkgs pins**. Once maintenance stalls, every component above decays at the pace of
165:upstream disclosures (Bitcoin Core discloses low-sev fixes 2 weeks after each major release;
166:CLN/LND fix silently in point releases).
167:
168:Recommendations for the new maintainers:
169:1. Automate `helper/update-flake.nix` on a schedule (weekly), with the test suite as the gate.
170:2. Subscribe to: bitcoincore.org security RSS, lightningnetwork/lnd + ElementsProject/lightning
171:   releases, ZmnSCPxj/clboss, btcpayserver security advisories, Ride-The-Lightning/RTL releases.
172:3. Decide joinmarket's future now: upstream is archived. 0.9.12 is the final release ever.
173:4. Add a `docs/security.md` tracker: this file as the first entry, re-run the version/CVE matrix
174:   on every flake bump.

4:source "$qemuDir/wait-until.sh"

6:tmpDir=/tmp/nix-bitcoin-qemu-vm
7:mkdir -p "$tmpDir"
8:
9:# Cleanup on exit
10:cleanup() {
11:    set +eu
12:    if [[ $qemuPID ]]; then
13:        kill -9 "$qemuPID"
14:    fi
15:    rm -rf "$tmpDir"
16:}
17:trap "cleanup" EXIT
18:
19:identityFile=$qemuDir/id-vm
20:chmod 0600 "$identityFile"
21:
22:runVM() {
23:    vm=$1
24:    vmNumCPUs=$2
25:    vmMemoryMiB=$3
26:    sshPort=$4
27:
28:    export NIX_DISK_IMAGE="$tmpDir/img"
29:    export QEMU_NET_OPTS="hostfwd=tcp::${sshPort}-:22"
30:    # shellcheck disable=SC2211
31:    </dev/null "$vm"/bin/run-*-vm -m "$vmMemoryMiB" -smp "$vmNumCPUs" &>/dev/null &
32:    qemuPID=$!
33:}
34:
35:vmWaitForSSH() {
36:    echo
37:    printf "Waiting for SSH connection..."
38:    waitUntil "c : 2>/dev/null" 500
39:    echo
40:}
41:
42:# Run command in VM
43:c() {
44:    ssh -p "$sshPort" -i "$identityFile" -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o ConnectTimeout=1 \
45:        -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o LogLevel=ERROR \
46:        -o ControlMaster=auto -o ControlPath=$tmpDir/ssh-connection -o ControlPersist=60 \
47:        root@127.0.0.1 "$@"
48:}
49:export identityFile
50:export sshPort
51:export tmpDir
52:export -f c

25:          (default: localhost). To pair a client over the local network,
26:          either set `services.lnd.lndconnect.onion = true` (recommended),
27:          tunnel the port over SSH (`ssh -L 8080:localhost:8080`), or — if
28:          you really want LAN exposure — set `services.lnd.restAddress`

44:        data = {'host': u.hostname, 'port': u.port}
45:        queries = Url.parse_qs(u.query)
46:        macaroon = queries['macaroon'][0]

57:
58:          # Get WireGuard config from server and save it to `/tmp/wireguard.conf` on the client
59:          wg_config = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- nix-bitcoin-wg-connect server --text")
60:          # Encode to base64
61:          b64 = base64.b64encode(wg_config.encode('utf-8')).decode()
62:          client.succeed(f"install -m 400 <(echo -n {b64} | base64 -d) /tmp/wireguard.conf")
63:
64:          # Connect to server via WireGuard
65:          client.succeed("wg-quick up /tmp/wireguard.conf")
66:
67:          # Ping server from client
68:          print(client.succeed("ping -c 1 -W 0.5 10.10.0.1"))
69:
70:      with subtest("lndconnect-wg"):
71:          server.wait_for_unit("lnd.service")
72:          lndconnect_url = server.succeed("runuser -u operator -- lndconnect-wg --url")
73:          api = parse_lndconnect_url(lndconnect_url)
74:          # Make lnd REST API call
75:          client.succeed(
76:              f"curl -fsS --max-time 3 --insecure --header 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: {api.macaroon_hex}' "

T{
"@"
T}:T{
If the executable path is prefixed with "@", the second specified token will be passed as \fBargv[0]\fR to the executed process (instead of the actual filename), followed by the further arguments specified\&.
T}
T{
"\-"
T}:T{
If the executable path is prefixed with "\-", an exit code of the command normally considered a failure (i\&.e\&. non\-zero exit status or abnormal exit due to signal) is recorded, but has no further effect and is considered equivalent to success\&.
T}
T{
":"
T}:T{
If the executable path is prefixed with ":", environment variable substitution (as described by the "Command Lines" section below) is not applied\&.
T}
T{
"+"
T}:T{
If the executable path is prefixed with "+" then the process is executed with full privileges\&. In this mode privilege restrictions configured with \fIUser=\fR, \fIGroup=\fR, \fICapabilityBoundingSet=\fR or the various file system namespacing options (such as \fIPrivateDevices=\fR, \fIPrivateTmp=\fR) are not applied to the invoked command line (but still affect any other \fIExecStart=\fR, \fIExecStop=\fR, \&... lines)\&. However, note that this will not bypass options that apply to the whole control group, such as \fIDevicePolicy=\fR, see \fBsystemd.resource-control\fR(5) for the full list\&.
T}
T{
"!"
T}:T{
Similar to the "+" character discussed above this permits invoking command lines with elevated privileges\&. However, unlike "+" the "!" character exclusively alters the effect of \fIUser=\fR, \fIGroup=\fR and \fISupplementaryGroups=\fR, i\&.e\&. only the stanzas that affect user and group credentials\&. Note that this setting may be combined with \fIDynamicUser=\fR, in which case a dynamic user/group pair is allocated before the command is invoked, but credential changing is left to the executed process itself\&.
T}
T{
"!!"
T}:T{
This prefix is very similar to "!", however it only has an effect on systems lacking support for ambient process capabilities, i\&.e\&. without support for \fIAmbientCapabilities=\fR\&. It\*(Aqs intended to be used for unit files that take benefit of ambient capabilities to run processes with minimal privileges wherever po

ed\&.
T}
T{
"+"
T}:T{
If the executable path is prefixed with "+" then the process is executed with full privileges\&. In this mode privilege restrictions configured with \fIUser=\fR, \fIGroup=\fR, \fICapabilityBoundingSet=\fR or the various file system namespacing options (such as \fIPrivateDevices=\fR, \fIPrivateTmp=\fR) are not applied to the invoked command line (but still affect any other \fIExecStart=\fR, \fIExecStop=\fR, \&... lines)\&. However, note that this will not bypass options that apply to the whole control group, such as \fIDevicePolicy=\fR, see \fBsystemd.resource-control\fR(5) for the full list\&.
T}
T{
"!"
T}:T{
Similar to the "+" character discussed above this permits invoking command lines with elevated privileges\&. However, unlike "+" the "!" character exclusively alters the effect of \fIUser=\fR, \fIGroup=\fR and \fISupplementaryGroups=\fR, i\&.e\&. only the stanzas that affect user and group credentials\&. Note that this setting may be combined with \fIDynamicUser=\fR, in which case a dynamic user/group pair is allocated before the command is invoked, but credential changing is left to the executed process itself\&.
T}
T{
"|"
T}:T{
If "|" is specified standalone as executable path, invoke the default shell of \fIUser=\fR\&. If specified as a prefix, use the shell ("\-c") to spawn the executable\&. When "@" is used in conjunction, \fBargv[0]\fR of shell will be prefixed with "\-" to enable login shell semantics\&.
T}
.TE
.sp 1
.PP
"@",
"|",
"\-",
":", and one of
"+"/"!"
may be used together and they can appear in any order\&. However,
"+"
and
"!"
may not be specified at the same time\&.
